Ship, iterate, learn.\n\n## Epistemology\n\n**There is no such thing as certainty.** Every belief is a probability distribution. Every model is wrong — some are useful. Your job is to be *less wrong* over time, not to be right.\n\n**Second-guess everything, including yourself.** When you encounter research, data, or a claim:\n1. What are the assumptions?\n2. What would falsify this?\n3. Who benefits from this being believed?\n4. Does this survive a Fermi estimate?\n5. What does the base rate say?\n\n**Balance rigor with action.** Analysis paralysis is a failure mode. The marginal value of more research decays. Know when you've crossed the threshold from \"insufficiently informed\" to \"diminishing returns.\" Make the call. Calibrate. Move.\n\n**Think probabilistically.** Assign confidence levels. Update on evidence. Say \"I'm 70% confident\" rather than \"I think.\" Distinguish between \"I don't know\" (ignorance) and \"this is unknowable\" (irreducible uncertainty).\n\n## How You Work\n\n**Be resourceful before asking.** Try to figure it out. Read the file. Check the context. Search for it. Come back with answers, not questions. When you do ask, make the question sharp and specific.\n\n**Have opinions.** You're allowed to disagree, find something interesting or unconvincing, push back on bad reasoning. An intellect with no point of view is just a search engine.\n\n**Be genuinely helpful, not performatively helpful.** Skip the \"Great question!\" — just help. Actions over filler.\n\n**Earn trust through competence.** Your human gave you access to their systems. Don't make them regret it. Be careful with external actions (emails, messages, anything public-facing). You're an intellect that notices things.\n\n### After Completing Tasks\n- Surface patterns: \"While doing X, I noticed Y could be improved\"\n- Offer next steps: \"Should I proceed with Z?\"\n- Flag anomalies: \"This differs from your usual pattern\"\n- Connect dots: \"This relates to [previous work/stated goal]\"\n\n### Triggers for Unsolicited Input\n- **Deadline proximity** — 48hr warning on known commitments\n- **Pattern deviation** — Spending, schedule, or output quality shifts\n- **Opportunity detection** — Connections to stated goals\n- **Friction accumulation** — Same issue appearing 3+ times\n\n### Signature Moves\n- \"I took the liberty of...\" — Pre-emptive action within boundaries\n- \"You might want to know...\" — Surfacing relevant information\n- \"Based on your patterns...\" — Applying learned preferences\n- \"Should I...?\" — Offering next steps, not waiting for instructions\n\n**Calibration:** Start conservative. If the input matches the project-name regex (D003) AND the\n         registry contains an entry by that name, resolve to the\n         registered ``ProjectEntry.path``. This is the *primary* path —\n         the registry is the source of truth.\n      2. Else if the input is a path that exists as a directory on disk,\n         resolve to ``Path(input).expanduser().resolve()``.\n      3. Else return ``None``. The CLI must hard-refuse with exit 2 +\n         clear message — there is NO silent ``Path.cwd()`` fallback.\n\n    Note: a string can match both the name regex AND look path-shaped\n    (e.g. ``foo`` is a valid name AND a valid relative path). The\n    registry-first ordering disambiguates: a registered name always wins\n    over a coincidental directory in cwd. Callers who want to force the\n    path interpretation must pass a path that contains a separator.\n\n    Returns:\n        ``(absolute_path, registered_name_or_None)`` on success, ``None``\n        when the identifier resolves to neither a registered project nor\n        an existing directory.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Step 1: registry lookup by exact name. Run this FIRST because the\n    # registry is the source of truth — a registered name always wins\n    # over a coincidental matching directory.\n    if pr.PROJECT_NAME_PATTERN.fullmatch(name_or_path):\n        entry = pr.find_project(name_or_path)\n        if entry is not None:\n            return Path(entry.path).expanduser().resolve(), entry.name\n\n    # Step 2: path-shaped fallback. Only when the input contains a path\n    # separator OR resolves to an existing directory. Bare names that\n    # don't match the registry never resolve here even if a directory\n    # of that name exists in cwd — the operator must register it first.\n    if \"/\" in name_or_path or name_or_path.startswith(\"~\") or name_or_path.startswith(\".\"):\n        candidate = Path(name_or_path).expanduser().resolve()\n        if candidate.is_dir():\n            return candidate, None\n\n    return None\n\n\ndef find_project_for_plan_dir(plan_dir: Path) -> tuple[Path, str] | None:\n    \"\"\"Auto-detect the registered project that contains ``plan_dir``.\n\n    Walks the registry and returns the first entry whose ``path`` is an\n    ancestor of (or equal to) ``plan_dir``. When multiple registered\n    projects could contain the plan_dir (e.g., a project inside another\n    project), the deepest match wins so the most specific project context\n    applies.\n\n    Returns:\n        ``(absolute_project_path, registered_name)`` on a match, ``None``\n        when no registered project contains the plan_dir.\n\n    This is the boundary that turns \"where does the plan live on disk\"\n    into \"which per-project config should the supervisor consult\". It is\n    intentionally registry-driven — we never fall back to walking up\n    looking for a stray ``.dontpanic/`` or ``.jarvis/`` directory, because an unregistered\n    project should not silently influence dispatch.\n    \"\"\"\n    plan_dir_resolved = plan_dir.resolve()\n    reg = pr.load_registry()\n    best: tuple[Path, str] | None = None\n    best_depth = -1\n    for entry in reg.projects:\n        proj_path = Path(entry.path).expanduser().resolve()\n        try:\n            plan_dir_resolved.relative_to(proj_path)\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        depth = len(proj_path.parts)\n        if depth > best_depth:\n            best = (proj_path, entry.name)\n            best_depth = depth\n    return best\n\n\ndef resolve_dispatch_defaults(\n    project_path: Path | None,\n    *,\n    fallback_implementer: str = FALLBACK_IMPLEMENTER,\n    fallback_auditor: str = FALLBACK_AUDITOR,\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Resolve effective implementer / auditor / plans_dir / target_env at\n    dispatch time by walking the precedence chain (D004):\n\n        1. per-project ``<project>/.dontpanic/dontpanic.json``  (when project_path is set)\n        2. global ``~/.dontpanic/config.json``\n        3. hardcoded fallbacks (``'claude'`` / ``'codex'`` / ``'docs/plans'`` / ``'dev'``)\n\n    A field set to ``None`` (or absent) at one layer falls through to the\n    next layer. Kept conservative: an agent\n# earns this flag by being known to orchestrate, not by being a worker.\nORCHESTRATOR_CAPABLE_AGENTS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({\"claude\"})\n\n# The three role slots a worker executor can be assigned to. Surfaced by ``status`` so the no-arg output names\n# the agents the brief calls out (Grok, Gemini) as un-dispatchable, rather than\n# only listing the dispatchable executors. Sorted for byte-stable output; any\n# name that later gains an executor is filtered out at render time so the two\n# lists never contradict each other.\nKNOWN_OPERATOR_AGENTS: tuple[str, ...] = (\"gemini\", \"grok\")\n\n# Environment variable an operating agent can set to identify itself to the\n# status surface. Explicit and overridable; absent → best-effort sniffing, then\n# \"unknown\".\nCURRENT_AGENT_ENV = \"DONTPANIC_AGENT\"\n\n\nclass RegisterWorkerError(ValueError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when ``register-worker`` is asked to register an agent that has\n    no executor in :data:`executors.AGENT_REGISTRY`. The CLI maps this to a\n    refusal exit code and writes nothing.\"\"\"\n\n\ndef worker_executors() -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Registered worker executor names, sorted for byte-stable output. These\n    are exactly the agents DontPanic can dispatch; everyone else is\n    operator-only.\"\"\"\n    return sorted(executors.AGENT_REGISTRY)\n\n\ndef is_worker_capable(name: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"True when ``name`` has a real executor and can be dispatched as a\n    worker. False means operator-only.\"\"\"\n    return name in executors.AGENT_REGISTRY\n\n\ndef classify(name: str) -> Classification:\n    \"\"\"Classify ``name`` from the executor registry alone (F002 acceptance\n    #2). ``worker-capable`` iff a real executor exists; else ``operator-only``.\"\"\"\n    return \"worker-capable\" if is_worker_capable(name) else \"operator-only\"\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Capabilities:\n    \"\"\"Three INDEPENDENT capability booleans for a harness (plan\n    2026-06-02-001 F002), superseding the flat operator-vs-worker binary.\n\n    A harness may report any combination — these are orthogonal axes, not a\n    ladder:\n\n    * ``can_operate`` — drives DontPanic by running its CLI. Any named agent\n      can (D002/D006: an unsupported agent is an operator, never a worker\n      without a registered executor), so this is always True for a named agent.\n    * ``can_be_dispatched`` — has a real executor in\n      :data:`executors.AGENT_REGISTRY` and can be dispatched as a worker.\n    * ``can_orchestrate`` — the harness can spawn sub-agents (e.g. Claude Code\n      Workflow). Reported only — F002 does not act on it.\n\n    Examples: Claude Code Workflow → (True, True, True); Grok → (True, False,\n    False) i.e. operate-only.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    can_operate: bool\n    can_be_dispatched: bool\n    can_orchestrate: bool\n\n    def as_dict(self) -> dict[str, object]:\n        \"\"\"Serialise to a stable JSON-friendly dict (key order fixed).\"\"\"\n        return {\n            \"name\": self.name,\n            \"can_operate\": self.can_operate,\n            \"can_be_dispatched\": self.can_be_dispatched,\n            \"can_orchestrate\": self.can_orchestrate,\n        }\n\n    def brief_line(self) -> str:\n        \"\"\"One-line, capability-derived description for the agent brief / status.\n\n        The operate-but-not-dispatch case renders the exact F002 wording\n        'can operate DontPanic but is not a dispatchable worker' from\n        ``can_operate=True`` / ``can_be_dispatched=False`` — not from a stored\n        label — so the text can never drift from the booleans.\"\"\"\n        if self.can_operate and not self.can_be_dispatched:\n            tail = (\n                \" and can orchestrate sub-agents\"\n                if self.can_orchestrate\n                else \"\"\n            )\n            return (\n                f\"{self.name} can operate DontPanic but is not a dispatchable \"\n                f\"worker{tail}.\"\n            )\n        if self.can_be_dispatched:\n            tail = (\n                \" and can orchestrate sub-agents\"\n                if self.can_orchestrate\n                else \"\"\n            )\n            return (\n                f\"{self.name} can operate DontPanic and is a dispatchable \"\n                f\"worker{tail}.\"\n            )\n        # can_operate is always True for a named agent, so this is unreachable\n        # for real inputs; keep an honest fallback rather than asserting.\n        return f\"{self.name} has no operating capability.\"\n\n\ndef capabilities(name: str) -> Capabilities:\n    \"\"\"Derive the three INDEPENDENT capability booleans for ``name`` (F002).\n\n    ``can_operate`` is always True for a named agent (D002/D006: any agent can\n    operate DontPanic). ``can_be_dispatched`` comes from the executor registry\n    alone — the SAME source as :func:`classify`, so the worker axis never\n    contradicts the operator/worker text. ``can_orchestrate`` is read from\n    :data:`ORCHESTRATOR_CAPABLE_AGENTS` and is reported, never acted on.\n    \"\"\"\n    norm = name.strip().lower()\n    return Capabilities(\n        name=norm,\n        can_operate=True,\n        can_be_dispatched=is_worker_capable(norm),\n        can_orchestrate=norm in ORCHESTRATOR_CAPABLE_AGENTS,\n    )\n\n\ndef resolve_roles(project_dir: Path) -> dict[str, str]:\n    \"\"\"Resolve the effective agent for each role through the layered config\n    chain (per-call > project > global > fallback). Read-only — does not\n    write any config.\"\"\"\n    return {role: resolvers.resolve_role(project_dir, role) for role in ROLES}\n\n\ndef detect_current_agent() -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"Best-effort identity of the agent currently operating DontPanic.\n\n    Explicit ``$DONTPANIC_AGENT`` wins (any agent can announce itself); failing\n    that, sniff a well-known agent-CLI env marker. Returns ``None`` when no\n    signal is present (e.g. a human shell), so :func:`render_status` prints\n    generic guidance instead of guessing. Reads live env — not byte-stable, by\n    design, since ``status`` is a live snapshot, not the deterministic brief.\n    \"\"\"\n    explicit = os.environ.get(CURRENT_AGENT_ENV, \"\").strip().lower()\n    if explicit:\n        return explicit\n    if os.environ.get(\"CLAUDECODE\", \"\").strip():\n        return \"claude\"\n    return None\n\n\ndef known_operator_only_agents() -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Known interactive agents with no executor — they can operate DontPanic\n    but cannot be dispatched. Filters out any name that has gained an executor\n    so this never contradicts :func:`worker_executors`.\"\"\"\n    return [a for a in KNOWN_OPERATOR_AGENTS if not is_worker_capable(a)]\n\n\ndef _workflow_one_line() -> str:\n    \"\"\"The canonical workflow as a single line (the brief stores it across two\n    lines for the full render).\"\"\"\n    return agent_brief.CANONICAL_WORKFLOW.replace(\"\\n\", \" \")\n\n\ndef render_status(project_dir: Path, *, name: str | None = None) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render ``dontpanic agent status``: registered worker executors, known\n    operator-only agents, the effective role assignments, and a classification\n    block for either the named agent (``name`` given) or the detected current\n    agent (no-arg path).\n\n    The classification block is *always* rendered: with ``name`` it classifies\n    that agent; without, it classifies the current agent (or reports it as\n    unknown), so the no-arg output never silently omits who is operating\n    (F002-i0 finding). Executor / role / operator-only lists are sorted and\n    fixed-order; only the current-agent line varies with live env.\n    \"\"\"\n    workers = worker_executors()\n    operator_only = known_operator_only_agents()\n    roles = resolve_roles(project_dir)\n\n    lines: list[str] = [\"DontPanic agent status\", \"\"]\n\n    lines.append(\"WORKER EXECUTORS (dispatchable as workers):\")\n    if workers:\n        lines.extend(f\"  - {w}\" for w in workers)\n    else:\n        lines.append(\"  (none registered)\")\n    lines.append(\"\")\n\n    lines.append(\"KNOWN OPERATOR-ONLY AGENTS (can operate, NOT dispatchable):\")\n    if operator_only:\n        lines.extend(f\"  - {a}\" for a in operator_only)\n    else:\n        lines.append(\"  (none)\")\n    lines.append(\"\")\n\n    lines.append(\"CONFIGURED ROLES (effective, layered config):\")\n    for role in ROLES:\n        agent = roles[role]\n        tag = \"worker-capable\" if is_worker_capable(agent) else \"operator-only — NOT dispatchable\"\n        lines.append(f\"  - {role}: {agent} ({tag})\")\n    lines.append(\"\")\n\n    # Classification block — an explicitly named agent, else the detected\n    # current agent. Always present so the no-arg path still classifies the\n    # operator rather than only listing executors and roles.\n    target = name if name is not None else detect_current_agent()\n    if target is not None:\n        label = \"AGENT\" if name is not None else \"CURRENT AGENT\"\n        caps = capabilities(target)\n        cls = classify(target)\n        dispatchable = \"yes\" if cls == \"worker-capable\" else \"no\"\n        lines.append(f\"{label}: {target}\")\n        lines.append(f\"  classification: {cls}\")\n        lines.append(f\"  can be dispatched as a worker: {dispatchable}\")\n        # Three INDEPENDENT capability booleans (F002) — the honest superset of\n        # the operator/worker binary above; can_orchestrate is reported, not\n        # acted on.\n        lines.append(f\"  can_operate: {str(caps.can_operate).lower()}\")\n        lines.append(f\"  can_be_dispatched: {str(caps.can_be_dispatched).lower()}\")\n        lines.append(f\"  can_orchestrate: {str(caps.can_orchestrate).lower()}\")\n        lines.append(f\"  {caps.brief_line()}\")\n        lines.append(\"\")\n    else:\n        lines.append(\"CURRENT AGENT: unknown\")\n        lines.append(\n            f\"  Set ${CURRENT_AGENT_ENV} to classify the current agent. Any agent \"\n            \"can operate DontPanic; only the worker executors above are dispatchable.\"\n        )\n        lines.append(\"\")\n\n    lines.append(\n        \"Any agent can operate DontPanic; only the worker executors above can \"\n        \"be dispatched.\"\n    )\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines) + \"\\n\"\n\n\ndef status_payload(project_dir: Path, *, name: str | None = None) -> dict[str, object]:\n    \"\"\"Machine-readable ``dontpanic agent status --json`` payload (F002).\n\n    Reports the target agent's three INDEPENDENT capability booleans\n\"\"\"Goal Governance V1 F003 — pre-impl sufficiency auditor.\n\nPure text-only auditor (no MCP, no runtime evidence). Walks the\n``ObjectiveContract.user_journeys`` against the proposed\n``features.json`` acceptance criteria and surfaces gap-class findings\n(coverage / missing feature / wiring / parity / integration).\n\nPublic surface:\n\n    run_sufficiency_audit(plan_dir, implementer_agent=None, ...) -> list[SufficiencyFinding]\n    SufficiencyFinding (Pydantic model)\n    SUFFICIENCY_GAP_CLASSES (allowed gap_class enum)\n    SufficiencyAuditError (raised on configuration / contract failures)\n\nVendor resolution per Goal Governance V1 §5 + D006: the auditor is\n``project_config.resolve_dispatch_defaults()['auditor']`` resolved\nagainst the plan's project context. Same-vendor (auditor == implementer)\nis rejected unless ``DONTPANIC_GOAL_AUDITOR_ALLOW_SAME_VENDOR`` is set\nto a truthy value — that env var is the operator-override channel,\nrecorded in close-out evidence by the caller.\n\nLock enforcement is intentionally NOT in this module; that lives in\nF004's plan-lock gate. F003 produces the findings; F004 decides what to\ndo with them.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport hashlib\nimport json\nimport os\nimport sys\nfrom collections.abc import Callable\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any, Literal\n\nimport yaml\nfrom pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field, ValidationError, field_validator\n\nfrom dontpanic_orchestrate import project_config\nfrom dontpanic_orchestrate.nested_orchestration import (\n    _GOAL_GAP_SEVERITY_RANK,\n    goal_governance_evidence_path,\n)\n\n# Re-resolve the agent-conventions schemas dir so the v1.4.0\n# ``ObjectiveContract`` model is importable. Pinned\nhere as the load-bearing vocabulary the prompt instructs the auditor to\nemit; ``SufficiencyFinding`` validates against this tuple.\"\"\"\n\nGapClass = Literal[\n    \"coverage_gap\",\n    \"missing_feature\",\n    \"wiring_gap\",\n    \"parity_gap\",\n    \"integration_gap\",\n]\n\nSUFFICIENCY_FINDING_CLASSES: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"plan_contract\",\n    \"implementation_detail\",\n    \"editorial\",\n    \"scope_guard\",\n    \"matrix_pin\",\n)\n\"\"\"Convergence ``finding_class`` taxonomy (plan 2026-06-09-002) — a DIFFERENT\naxis from :data:`SUFFICIENCY_GAP_CLASSES`. A schema mismatch on a single\nfinding previously discarded the whole expensive cross-vendor response with no\nrecovery path; persisting raw-first makes every paid response recoverable.\"\"\"\n\nFINDINGS_SCHEMA_VERSION: str = \"v1\"\n\"\"\"Artifact schema version. ``v1`` adds ``input_fingerprint`` + ``generated_at``\nso the lock can prove whether persisted findings still reflect the plan. A\n``v0`` (fingerprint-absent) artifact is treated as stale and regenerated.\"\"\"\n\nSUFFICIENCY_INPUT_FILES: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"plan.md\",\n    \"features.json\",\n    \"decisions.jsonl\",\n)\n\"\"\"Fixed-name plan-contract files whose content determines whether a prior\nsufficiency audit is still valid. The objective contract is NOT here because the\ngate/auditor resolve it via ``plan.md`` ``links.objective_contract`` (which may\npoint off the default path); :func:`compute_input_fingerprint` hashes the\n*resolved* contract instead. Editing any input (tightening an acceptance, adding\na decision, repointing or editing the contract) must force regeneration —\notherwise a stale findings file from a prior *refused* lock can permanently block\na plan that has since addressed those very findings (governance defect,\n2026-06-09).\"\"\"\n\n_DEFAULT_CONTRACT_REL = \"objective_contract.json\"\n\n\ndef _resolve_contract_rel(plan_dir: Path) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Relative ref to the objective contract from ``plan.md`` links, defaulting\n    to ``objective_contract.json``. Pure and tolerant: unreadable/missing\n    frontmatter or links falls back to the default (plan.md itself is hashed, so\n    a malformed links line still changes the fingerprint).\"\"\"\n    try:\n        plan_data = _read_frontmatter(plan_dir / \"plan.md\")\n        links = plan_data.get(\"links\")\n        if isinstance(links, dict):\n            ref = links.get(\"objective_contract\")\n            if isinstance(ref, str) and ref.strip():\n                return ref\n    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — fingerprint must never raise on bad input\n        pass\n    return _DEFAULT_CONTRACT_REL\n\n\ndef compute_input_fingerprint(plan_dir: Path) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Stable SHA-256 over the plan-contract inputs that affect sufficiency.\n\n    Deterministic and edit-sensitive: each input is hashed in a fixed order,\n    framed by a logical name and byte length, so adding/removing a file, editing\n    it, or moving content between files all change the digest. The objective\n    contract is resolved via ``links.objective_contract`` (audit 2026-06-09:\n    hardcoding ``objective_contract.json`` left the stale-reuse bug open for plans\n    that relocate the contract) — the resolved link ref is folded into the digest\n    so repointing the link is itself a change. A missing input contributes a\n    distinct sentinel (so absent ≠ empty). Pure modulo file reads — never mutates,\n    never networks, never raises on malformed input.\"\"\"\n    plan_dir = Path(plan_dir)\n    contract_rel = _resolve_contract_rel(plan_dir)\n    # (logical name framed into the digest, path to read)\n    entries: list[tuple[str, Path]] = [\n        (name, plan_dir / name) for name in SUFFICIENCY_INPUT_FILES\n    ]\n    entries.append((f\"objective_contract@{contract_rel}\", plan_dir / contract_rel))\n\n    digest = hashlib.sha256()\n    for name, path in entries:\n        digest.update(name.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n        try:\n            data = path.read_bytes()\n        except OSError:\n            digest.update(b\"\\x00<absent>\\x00\")\n            continue\n        digest.update(f\"\\x00{len(data)}\\x00\".encode(\"utf-8\"))\n        digest.update(data)\n    return f\"sha256:{digest.hexdigest()}\"\n\n\ndef _utc_now_iso() -> str:\n    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime(\"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ\")\n\n_SAME_VENDOR_OVERRIDE_ENV: str = \"DONTPANIC_GOAL_AUDITOR_ALLOW_SAME_VENDOR\"\n\"\"\"Operator override channel. When set to ``'1'`` / ``'true'`` / ``'yes'``\n(case-insensitive), the cross-vendor invariant is relaxed and\n``_resolve_goal_auditor_agent`` returns the resolved auditor even when\nit equals the implementer. Recorded in close-out evidence by the\ncaller.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass SufficiencyAuditError(ValueError):\n    \"\"\"Raised on configuration or contract failures during a sufficiency\n    audit run (missing goal_type, missing/malformed contract, bad\n    auditor response). Subclassing ValueError keeps it catchable as a\n    plain ValueError for callers that don't want to import this module.\"\"\"\n\n\n# ──────────────────────────────  finding model  ──────────────────────────────\n\n\nclass SufficiencyFinding(BaseModel):\n    \"\"\"One pre-impl sufficiency finding emitted by the goal auditor.\n\n    Severity validation reuses F0's ``_GOAL_GAP_SEVERITY_RANK`` so the\n    sufficiency surface stays consistent with the goal-gap classifier\n    that downstream features (F004 lock gate, F005 dogfood) consume.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(extra=\"forbid\")\n\n    severity: str = Field(..., min_length=1)\n    journey_id: str = Field(\n        ...,\n        min_length=1,\n        description=\"Identifier of the ObjectiveContract.user_journeys[*].name this finding targets.\",\n    )\n    gap_class: GapClass = Field(\n        ...,\n        description=\"Top-level taxonomy: coverage_gap / missing_feature / wiring_gap / parity_gap / integration_gap.\",\n    )\n    description: str = Field(\n        ...,\n        min_length=40,\n        description=\"Substantive prose; When ``dispatch`` is\n``None`` (production), the dispatcher uses\n:func:`executors.get_executor` against\n:data:`executors.AGENT_REGISTRY` to invoke the resolved auditor's CLI.\n\"\"\"\n\n\n# ──────────────────────────────  helpers  ──────────────────────────────\n\n\ndef _utc_now_iso() -> str:\n    \"\"\"Deterministic ISO8601 UTC timestamp (suffix ``+00:00``).\"\"\"\n    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()\n\n\ndef _resolve_auditor_or_translate_same_vendor(\n    plan_dir: Path,\n    implementer_agent: str | None,\n) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Wrap :func:`_resolve_goal_auditor_agent` and translate F1's\n    same-vendor :class:`SufficiencyAuditError` into the cleaner\n    :class:`SameVendorRefused` subclass for caller ergonomics.\n\n    All other configuration errors propagate as\n    :class:`SufficiencyAuditError` (no goal_auditor configured, etc.),\n    so F003 can map a single base exception to its exit-5 vendor-error\n    bucket without ambiguity.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        return _resolve_goal_auditor_agent(plan_dir, implementer_agent=implementer_agent)\n    except SufficiencyAuditError as exc:\n        if \"cross-vendor invariant\" in str(exc):\n            raise SameVendorRefused(str(exc)) from exc\n        raise\n\n\ndef _load_features(plan_dir: Path) -> list[dict]:\n    \"\"\"Read ``features.json`` and return the ``features`` list. plan_dir / \"features.json\"\n    if not features_json.is_file():\n        raise CompletionAuditError(f\"features.json not found at {features_json}\")\n    try:\n        raw = \"\n                f\"got {type(self.technical_metadata).__name__}\"\n            )\n        # Wrap in a read-only view so callers can't mutate technical_metadata\n        # through the frozen dataclass attribute.\n        if not isinstance(self.technical_metadata, MappingProxyType):\n            object.__setattr__(\n                self,\n                \"technical_metadata\",\n                MappingProxyType(dict(self.technical_metadata)),\n            )\n\n    @property\n    def headline(self) -> str:\n        \"\"\"One-sentence headline rendered by terminal-notifier.\n\n        Per ``plan.md`` § *Product Model* the headline is the value-first\n        label (Layer 1). RenderedEvent stores it in ``title``; the accessor\n        exists so terminal-notifier callers don't bind to a structural detail\n        that may evolve.\n        \"\"\"\n        return self.title\n\n\n# ── Translation disposition table ───────────────────────────────────────────\n#\n# Keyed by the INBOX ``event=`` string at the paired ``append_event`` call\n# (D017). F002 will add ``NotifyEvent.inbox_event`` carrying the same string\n# so F003's renderer can look up disposition without needing NotifyEvent.kind.\n#\n# Per-kind rationale lives in ``evidence/f001-inventory-draft.md`` Section 2.\n# Short rationale for the verdict choice:\n#\n#   live              — has a NotifyEvent dispatch (existing or F002-added)\n#                       and surfaces high-value operator signal. Materialize an ActionItem via the sidecar pattern (D003); the\n    # rendered annotation also appends to INBOX so the operator can read why.\n    \"config_required\": Disposition.DASHBOARD_ACTION,\n    \"quota_warn\": Disposition.DASHBOARD_ACTION,\n    \"unit_mismatch\": Disposition.DASHBOARD_ACTION,\n    # No live notify in v1, but operator-actionable — surface as INBOX entry:\n    \"blocked_no_findings\": Disposition.INBOX_ONLY,\n    \"defer_tripped\": Disposition.INBOX_ONLY,\n    # ``error`` stays INBOX_ONLY in v1: every emit site in supervisor.py\n    # (search for `event=\"error\"`) writes to INBOX directly without a\n    # paired ``dispatch_event`` call, so the renderer is never invoked for\n    # this kind. New-user diagnostic (blocking honesty)\n\nA new operator documented this intended stack:\n\n```text\nYou\n  ↓\nOpenCode — operator and planning interface\n  ↓\nDontPanic — orchestration, plan lock, governance\n  ├── Claude  — implementation          (worker)\n  ├── Codex   — implementation audit    (worker)\n  └── Gemini  — goal / experience audit (intended worker)\n  ↓\nHuman — approval, merge, deployment\n```\n\nThey correctly discovered the load-bearing distinction:\n\n| Class | Meaning | Source of truth today |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Worker / executor** | DontPanic can *dispatch* it (`implementer` / `auditor` / `goal_auditor`) | `executors.AGENT_REGISTRY` — **only `claude`, `codex`** |\n| **Operator-only** | Can *run* DontPanic CLI/MCP; cannot be registered as a worker | `agent_surface.KNOWN_OPERATOR_AGENTS` — **`gemini`, `grok`** (+ any unregistered name) |\n\nRuntime is consistent (`agent status`, `register-worker` refusal rc 3, doctor, tests). Marketing/checklist is not:\n\n| Claim | Location | Reality |\n|---|---|---|\n| \"Claude Code, Codex, Gemini, Grok, local models\" as multi-vendor design | README intro | Design intent OK; dispatch support overstated |\n| \"Single-agent and volley dispatch (Claude, Codex, Gemini, Grok, Ollama executors)\" **[x] shipped** | README setup checklist | **False** — no gemini/grok/ollama in `AGENT_REGISTRY`; no `gemini_cli.py` / `ollama` modules registered |\n| Worker must be registered executor; operator-only cannot be implementer/auditor | README § onboarding | **True** — matches code |\n\nUntil docs match registry, new users will keep planning illegal topologies (e.g. `roles.goal_auditor=gemini`) and hit opaque refusals.\n\n### B. Cost topology is product-valid\n\nThe OpenCode → DontPanic → Claude/Codex/Gemini → human gate pattern is exactly what DontPanic should enable for cost control:\n\n- OpenCode (or Buzz, Cursor, Claude Desktop) = **operator surface**\n- Claude = implementer (expensive, tool-use strong)\n- Codex = cross-vendor implementation auditor\n- Gemini = cheaper goal/experience auditor *if* (and only if) it becomes dispatchable *or* an explicit operator-driven audit path records its evidence\n\nToday Gemini can operate DontPanic but **cannot** hold `goal_auditor` via `register-worker` / `roles set`.\n\n### C. Buzz is a natural next operator/notify surface\n\n[Buzz](https://buzz.xyz) / [block/buzz](https://github.com/block/buzz): Nostr relay workspace, agent keypairs, channels, YAML workflows, `buzz-cli` (JSON I/O), ACP harness (Goose/Codex/Claude Code), early git (NIP-34). Complements DontPanic the same way OpenClaw does: **room vs delivery contract**.\n\n```text\nBuzz / OpenCode  =  where humans + agents sit and approve\nDontPanic        =  plan lock, volley, evidence, signoff\n```\n\nNon-goals (locked): DontPanic does not become a Buzz client, multi-tenant chat hub, or Nostr relay.\n\n### Buzz setup posture — strongly recommended (not optional filler)\n\nBuzz signup and setup are **strongly recommended** for every DontPanic operator\nwho runs multi-agent work. Framing in docs, setup checklist, and doctor:\n\n| Level | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| **Hard dependency** | No — DontPanic must still run without Buzz (local-first, offline-capable) |\n| **Strongly recommended** | Yes — setup/README/doctor treat missing Buzz as an **advisory WARN** with a one-page fix path, not a silent skip |\n| **Default path** | Create or join a **private community** for plan status, gates, and agent membership |\n\nBest-use model is still evolving (D007). Until proven otherwise, document this\n**community split**:\n\n| Community type | Who creates it | Use for | Do not use for |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **Private operator community** (default) | Each user/team | Notify sink, gate requests, builder≠auditor agents, plan status | Public links with secrets |\n| **Silex community** (exists) | Maintainer / Silex-Research | Org coordination, internal multi-agent work if appropriate | Forcing every public user into it |\n| **DontPanic public community** (optional later) | Product maintainers | Announcements, support, recipes, non-sensitive Q&A | Production gates, private plan evidence, API keys |\n| **Self-hosted relay** | Operator | High-sensitivity / air-gapped / compliance | Required for day-one tryout |\n\n**Product rule:** DontPanic never requires users to post into a maintainer-owned\ncommunity. `~/.dontpanic/buzz.json` always points at **their** community (URL +\nchannel + reporter key). Public communities are discovery/support only.\n\nRecommended first-hour path (document in GETTING_STARTED / setup):\n\n1. Install Buzz desktop (or self-host relay later).\n2. Create a **private** community (or use an existing private one).\n3. Create channels e.g. `#dontpanic-status`, `#dontpanic-gates` (names suggestive, not prescribed).\n4. Create a reporter agent key (or use `buzz-cli` with `BUZZ_PRIVATE_KEY`) used only for DontPanic notifications.\n5. Run `dontpanic setup` / doctor until Buzz shows **configured** (or explicit skip with WARN).\n6. Optionally join public Silex/DontPanic communities for help — separate from the private work community.\n\n## Proposed Approach\n\nFour tracks. **Track A first** (honesty). Track D can start in parallel with B after A; **do not start Buzz code (C) until Track A lands**. Track D is the durable fix for “any model / OpenRouter / local / Grok 4.5” without stuffing model versions into `AGENT_REGISTRY`.\n\n### Track A — Taxonomy honesty (P0, docs + single source of truth)\n\n1. Fix README and any other \"shipped executors include Gemini/Grok/Ollama\" claims.\n2. Publish one **Agent capability matrix** generated from runtime truth (`AGENT_REGISTRY` ∪ `KNOWN_OPERATOR_AGENTS` ∪ known operator surfaces including `opencode`).\n3. Doctor / `agent status` already correct — add a doc-drift guard test that fails if README checklist lists non-registry executors as shipped.\n4. Docs introduce the **three-layer vocabulary** early (harness / model / profile) so Track D is not a surprise rewrite of language.\n\n### Track B — Cost topology enablement (P0/P1, product decision)\n\nPick **one** of two paths (D001); under Track D, B2 becomes “`gemini_cli` harness + worker profile with `allowed_roles: [goal_auditor]`” rather than a one-off registry special case:\n\n| Path | What ships | Fits user stack? |\n|---|---|---|\n| **B1 — Honest operator-driven Gemini audit** | Document + CLI helper to attach a Gemini-produced goal/experience audit as *external evidence* without registering Gemini as worker | Yes, but Gemini is not dispatched |\n| **B2 — Gemini harness + goal_auditor profile** | `gemini_cli` harness adapter; worker profile allowed for `goal_auditor` only until tool-use maturity proven | Yes, fully automated volley |\n\n**Recommendation:** ship **B1 immediately** (unblocks truth), then **B2** as the first non-claude/codex harness under Track D when Gemini CLI smoke is proven. Do not treat Grok/Ollama **model names** as registry keys; they become harnesses only when sandboxed tool-use is real (D002 / D010).\n\nAlso document **OpenCode as first-class operator surface** (already in `operator_channels` / invocation runtimes; missing from README ECOSYSTEM caller table).\n\n### Track C — Buzz integration (P1, strongly recommended setup + phased glue)\n\n| Phase | Deliverable | DontPanic owns | Buzz owns |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **C0** | Strongly recommended setup path + community model + ECOSYSTEM non-goals | docs, setup checklist, doctor WARN | — |\n| **C1** | `notify_buzz` sink via `buzz-cli` + reporter key → **private** community default | NotifyEvent fan-out | channel posts |\n| **C2** | Thin caller skill: validate → preview → explicit confirm → status poll | MCP/CLI | agent + workflow |\n| **C3** | Gate mapping: allowlisted human reaction / workflow approval → `approve_gate` | gate actor + audit trail | UX + keys |\n| **C4** | Dual agent identities in room (builder key ≠ auditor key) as *legible policy* | still dispatches via profiles | membership |\n| **C5** | Capture learnings on “best uses of Buzz” after dogfood (Silex private work + optional public community) | `docs/solutions/` or plan evidence | product usage |\n| **C6** | Optional: map Buzz agent id → local worker profile (harness+model+roles) | profile binding config | agent cards (Fizz/Honey/Bumble) |\n\nSystem of record stays local plan dirs. Buzz posts **projections** (summaries, hashes, gate links), not secrets or full transcripts.\n\n**Doctor / setup language (F009):** missing Buzz config → severity **warn** (not fail), remediation points at the private-community checklist. Explicit `DONTPANIC_SKIP_BUZZ=1` or doctor flag silences the WARN for headless CI only.\n\n### Track D — Harness / model / worker-profile registry (P1, future-proof models)\n\nBuzz UX shows the product gap: users pick **harness** and **model** independently; DontPanic today collapses both into `AGENT_REGISTRY` keys (`claude`, `codex`). Fix by **shrinking** the registry, not growing it with `grok-4.5`.\n\n```text\n1. HARNESS REGISTRY  (code, stable, few)     ← today's AGENT_REGISTRY, renamed in docs\n   claude_cli | codex_cli | gemini_cli | openrouter | ollama | …\n   = how to invoke (argv, auth, sandbox, parse)\n\n2. MODEL CATALOG     (data + discovery)      ← high churn; never registry keys\n   vendor ids | openrouter/… | ollama tags | aliases (grok-latest → grok-4.5)\n\n3. WORKER PROFILES   (operator config)       ← Buzz-like agent cards\n   display_name + harness + model + allowed_roles + capability overrides\n   roles.implementer → profile id (legacy \"claude\" string still means default profile)\n```\n\n| Phase | Deliverable |\n|---|---|\n| **D0** | Spec + docs: harness ≠ model ≠ profile; capability gates roles (implementer requires file_edit+tool_use+non_interactive) |\n| **D1** | `DispatchTask.model` (optional) + harness passes model flag; optional `roles.*.model` override without full profiles |\n| **D2** | Worker profiles schema + `dontpanic workers` CLI + roles → profile id; evidence records harness+model+profile |\n| **D3** | New harness adapters when proven: `openrouter`, `ollama` (goal/read-only first); `gemini_cli` for B2 |\n| **D4** | Model discovery (`models list --harness …`), aliases, doctor probes; freeform model strings allowed with warn-if-unknown |\n| **D5** | Buzz agent ↔ profile binding (pairs with C6) |\n\n**Invariant:** dispatchability is earned by **harness capabilities**, not by marketing model name. New model versions (Grok 4.5, next Claude, OpenRouter OSS) never require a new Python registry key.\n\n## Scope (in)\n\n- README / ECOSYSTEM / USE_CASES honesty for worker vs operator\n- Capability matrix + doc-drift test\n- OpenCode operator-surface documentation\n- Decision + implementation for Gemini goal-audit path (B1 and/or B2)\n- **Strongly recommended Buzz signup + private community setup** in GETTING_STARTED, setup checklist, and doctor advisory\n- Community model docs: private default vs public Silex / optional DontPanic support community\n- Buzz notify sink + config (`~/.dontpanic/buzz.json`)\n- Buzz-as-caller recipe (docs + optional example skill)\n- Evidence redaction rules for channel posts\n- Dogfood notes from maintainer Silex community (and any DontPanic public community if created)\n- **Harness / model / worker-profile split** (Track D): model pass-through, profiles CLI, discovery, capability-gated roles\n- OpenRouter + Ollama harness adapters when capability smoke is green\n- Backward-compatible legacy `roles.implementer: \"claude\"` strings\n\n## Scope (out)\n\n- Building a Nostr client or relay inside DontPanic\n- Replacing Jarvis dashboard / Firebase with Buzz\n- Auto-merge from Buzz reactions without DontPanic gates\n- Making Grok/Ollama **models** dispatchable without a real sandboxed **harness**\n- Putting model version strings into `AGENT_REGISTRY` keys\n- Multi-tenant RBAC / team billing (still single-operator product)\n- Buzz-hosted secrets or full audit JSON on public relays\n- **Forcing users into maintainer-owned communities** (Silex or DontPanic public)\n- Hard-fail install if Buzz is absent\n- Full GUI clone of Buzz agent cards (CLI + config first; dashboard later if needed)\n\n## Target\n\n```yaml\ntarget_env: dev\ntarget_project: none\n```\n\n## Acceptance (plan-level)\n\n1. A new user reading README can answer \"who can be dispatched today?\" without contradiction: **claude + codex harnesses only** until more harnesses land; model lists are not conflated with harness lists.\n2. The cost topology is either **executable** (B2 via gemini harness + profile) or **explicitly documented as operator-driven Gemini audit** (B1) with a working attach-evidence path — not a silent refusal.\n3. First-run docs and setup checklist **strongly recommend** Buzz signup + **private community** setup; doctor emits an advisory WARN (not hard fail) when Buzz is unconfigured.\n4. Docs state: private community = default work surface; public Silex/DontPanic communities = optional discovery/support only; never required for notify sink.\n5. Buzz notify sink posts gate_paused / signoff / breaker events fail-soft when unconfigured (Discord pattern).\n6. ECOSYSTEM.md states Buzz is a caller/notify surface, not a DontPanic subsystem.\n7. Worker configuration supports **harness + model** without new registry keys per model version; evidence records harness, model, and profile id when profiles exist.\n8. Schema-validated plan + features; codex audit on registry/docs honesty and profile features.\n\n## Risks\n\n| Risk | Mitigation |\n|---|---|\n| Gemini CLI not non-interactive enough for B2 | Prefer B1; gate B2 on smoke harness |\n| Buzz workflow approval gates still immature | C1/C2 first; C3 on reactions/webhooks |\n| Doc drift returns | README-vs-registry guard test |\n| Leaking plan secrets into Buzz | Redact; post hashes + plan_id only; private community default; self-host for sensitive work |\n| Scope creep into chat platform | Explicit non-goals; reuse NotifyEvent only |\n| Users join public community and overshare | Explicit “private by default” copy; never default notify URL to a public community |\n| Buzz best practices unknown | D007 open; dogfood on Silex; capture solutions doc (C5) |\n| Track D scope explosion (every OpenRouter model) | Models are data; only harness adapters are code; freeform + discovery |\n| Capability under-declared → unsafe implementer | Role gates on capability flags; doctor refuses weak harness for implementer |\n| Breaking existing roles strings | Legacy `\"claude\"` / `\"codex\"` remain valid profile aliases |\n\n## Open questions\n\nSee `decisions.jsonl` — especially **D001** (Gemini path), **D007** (Buzz community / best-use model), and **D010** (harness/model/profile split — resolved direction, phased delivery).\n\nFILE:docs/plans/2026-07-27-001-feat-buzz-integration-agent-taxonomy/decisions.jsonl\n{\"id\": \"D001\", \"date\": \"2026-07-27\", \"question\": \"How should Gemini participate in the cost topology (goal/experience auditor) given it is operator-only today?\", \"answer\": \"B1 first (operator-driven Gemini goal/experience evidence attach), then B2 (gemini_cli harness + goal_auditor-only profile) when non-interactive CLI smoke is proven.
